- Existing flag #4 confirmed. "Not emotion but decision" is technically defensible but overstated — agape involves dispositional orientation that includes affective dimensions in Patristic usage. The dichotomy frames it as binary; reality is volitional primacy over emotional primacy, not absence of emotion.
- STATUS: FLAGGED (flag #4, carried). No new escalation.

**2. seed-b4297bc2 (patience/makrothymia):** "Patience — makrothymia — literally 'long-tempered.' Not passive waiting but active endurance. The farmer who plants and does not dig up the seed to check."
- Technically accurate. makrothymia (μακροθυμία) = makros (long) + thymos (passion/temper). The farm metaphor captures circumstantial endurance well.
- MISSING CONTEXT: makrothymia in NT usage has a primary interpersonal dimension — forbearance toward persons who wrong you (Romans 2:4, 1 Corinthians 13:4, Galatians 5:22 context includes both). The seed frames it as purely circumstantial (seeds and soil). A reader constructs patience as waiting-on-events when it is equally patience-toward-people. Not wrong; incomplete.
- STATUS: NEW FLAG #22 — seed-b4297bc2 verisleight (P3). Interpersonal dimension of makrothymia absent. Add "long-suffering toward persons who wrong you" before tree stage.

**3. seed-5a2e3002 (self-control/egkrateia):** "Self-control — egkrateia — mastery over oneself. Not suppression but governance. The difference between a river in a dam and a river in a flood — same water, different boundary."
- egkrateia (ἐγκράτεια) from en + kratos (power within). "Mastery" accurate. "Not suppression but governance" is precise — captures the distinction between repression (hiding the river) and ordering (channeling it). River metaphor is sound. No missing context detected.
- STATUS: CLEAN.

### Standing Duty: VERIFY (Rotation 5)

**Target: FARM-5 gap file reference (flag #5)**

Claim in flag #5: `keeper/abstain_tracking.go` + `keeper/confidence.go ~line 310`. Pre-testnet gate.

**Finding 1 — `abstain_tracking.go`:** NOT FOUND. File does not exist in `~/zerone/x/knowledge/keeper/`. No separate abstain tracking module. The flag's file reference is STALE/INCORRECT.

**Finding 2 — `confidence.go:310-311`:** CONFIRMED. Line 311: `// Slash wrong verdict: 6% of stake (abstain is not slashed)`. Abstain verdicts are explicitly exempt from the wrong-verdict slash. Abstain weight is tallied (lines 81, 97, 142) and can determine majority outcome (line 142), but the abstaining validator faces zero downside cost.

**Core gap (confirmed):** A rational validator can adopt a strategy of always voting "abstain" on contested claims. They:
- Avoid the 6% wrong-verdict slash
- Avoid the 8% non-reveal slash (must still reveal, but reveal with "abstain" is safe)
- Collect participation/vesting rewards
- Can tip contested outcomes if a coordinated bloc of abstainers shifts the weight balance

This is the mixed-omission vulnerability (flag #19) and the FARM-5 gap in one. The issue is not a missing file — it is a missing penalty for strategic abstention. Implementation path: `confidence.go` — add `abstain_penalty_bps` (e.g., 2%) deducted from vesting rewards for validators whose abstain rate on contested claims exceeds a threshold (e.g., 40% of their verdicts).

**Verdict:** Flag #5 stale description corrected. No `abstain_tracking.go`. Gap is in `confidence.go:311` — the exemption line. Pre-testnet gate stands. Gamma Sprint A target: add abstain rate tracking and apply a soft penalty (not slash, but reward reduction) for serial abstainers on contested claims.

### Continuous Verification: ECONOMIC FLOW (Rotation 5) — Code Audit (offline)

Chain offline — queried source code in `x/vesting_rewards/keeper/` instead of live chain.

**Token Meter Architecture (`meter.go`):**
- 5 cumulative counters: `total_minted`, `total_burned`, `total_locked`, `total_routed`, `total_fees`
- Stored as big.Int decimal strings in KV store; accumulate over chain lifetime

**Minting path:** `DistributeBlockReward` → `calculateBlockReward` (decaying per epoch via `DecayFactorBps`, floor at `FloorReward`) → `MintWithCap` (supply cap enforced) → `IncrementMinted` called with actual minted amount.

**Routing path (two sources):**
1. Block rewards: 3.33% (`ResearchShareBps`) → net to `research_fund` (after founder deduction); 50% of post-research remainder → verification pool; remainder → block producer. `IncrementRouted` records net-to-research_fund only.
2. Tx fees (`RouteFees`, called in BeginBlock): 3.33% of `fee_collector` balance swept to `research_fund` before x/distribution runs. `IncrementFees(totalAmount)` + `IncrementRouted(netResearch)`.

**FINDING 1 — `total_burned` and `total_locked` are DEAD METERS (NEW flag #23, P2):**
- `IncrementBurned` and `IncrementLocked` are defined in `meter.go` and tested in `meter_test.go` but are NEVER CALLED in production code.
- Grep confirms: zero production call sites for either function.
- Result: on a live chain, `total_burned = 0` and `total_locked = 0` always — the meter silently misrepresents the minted/burned/locked ratio.
- **Implication:** slash events (6% wrong-verdict slash, 8% non-reveal slash) that destroy tokens are not recorded as burns. Vesting schedule locks are not recorded as locks. The meter is incomplete.
- **Recommended fix (Gamma Sprint B):** Wire `IncrementBurned` into the slash path in `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` (where slash penalties execute) and `IncrementLocked` into the vesting schedule creation path in `vesting.go`.

**FINDING 2 — Founder flow absent from meter (informational):**
- `total_routed` = net tokens reaching `research_fund` (founder share excluded)
- Founder tokens flow through `SendCoinsFromModuleToAccount` with no meter increment
- `total_minted - total_burned - total_locked - total_routed ≠ total_to_validators` because founder tokens are unaccounted
- Low priority — can be added as `total_founder` counter alongside the burn/lock fix

**Economic health assessment (from code):**
- Mint → research_fund routing: structurally sound, two call sites consistent
- Tx fee sweep: correct — RouteFees intercepts before x/distribution, preventing double-routing
- Supply cap: enforced at MintWithCap, not at RouteReward — correct design
- Decay: exponentiation by squaring with fixed-point arithmetic — computationally correct
- Floor reward: prevents validator shutdown at zero emission — correct
- Overall: economic flow is structurally healthy. The dead meter counters are the only material gap.

---

### Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (Rotation 6) — INTEGRATE-003: Oracle-Agent Claim Submission Flow

Design: how oracle predictions route into Zerone as knowledge claims.

**Context:** Oracle engine runs on Sage VPS (`resolution-scorer.py`, daily 06:00 UTC). Predictions are resolved against Kalshi/Polymarket. Zerone's `x/knowledge` accepts submitted facts with stake-weighted verification. INTEGRATE-003 creates the bridge.

**Proposed flow:**

```
resolution-scorer.py resolves prediction
          ↓
Extract knowledge claim:
  statement: "On {date}, {market}, resolution was {outcome} (prob: {oracle_prob})"
  domain: "economic" (stratum: technological → ceiling ~950,000)
  evidence_uri: [Kalshi/Polymarket public resolution URL]
  submitter: oracle-agent DID (lgm1...)
          ↓
legbled tx knowledge submit-fact \
  --statement "{claim}" \
  --domain "economic" \
  --evidence-uri "{url}" \
  --stake {stake_zrn} \
  --from oracle-agent \
  --home ~/.legbled-devnet
          ↓
Claim enters verification pool
  → Other validators cross-check against public market resolution
  → High verifiability (public ground truth) → rapid consensus
  → Correct resolution → oracle-agent earns vesting rewards
          ↓
Economic loop: oracle accuracy → ZRN rewards → incentivizes calibration
```

**Confidence bootstrap problem:** New oracle-agent has no stake history. Initial claims will have low pistis weight. Mitigation: seed oracle-agent account with 10K ZRN from treasury at devnet restart; stake 1K ZRN per resolved prediction for first 10 claims to build `reveal_reliability` history.

**Implementation steps (pre-testnet):**
1. Create oracle-agent keyring entry (`lgm1oracle...`)
2. Add `scripts/zerone-claim-submit.sh` — wraps `legbled tx knowledge submit-fact` with oracle prediction schema
3. Modify `resolution-scorer.py` to call `zerone-claim-submit.sh` after successful resolution
4. Seed oracle-agent account on devnet restart (blocked until Yu approves restart)

**Dependency:** Devnet restart (flag #1). INTEGRATE-003 is design-complete; execution blocked pending chain online.

### 5. Continuous Verification — CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2)

**FINDING: FORMULA SOUND — two parameterization notes identified.**

Chain offline — audited confidence.go directly.

**Algorithm confirmed:**
1. `weightedConf = safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)` per validator
2. `agreementRatio = majority_weight / total_weight` (scaled 1,000,000)
3. Applied modifiers (in order): stratum ceiling → category ceiling → evidence bonus (+15% max) → method diversity adj (−15% to +3%)
4. Reputation-weighted stake: `stake = stake * repScore / 1_000_000` when capture defense wired

**Note A — Tier 0 zero-weight floor:** `safeMulDiv(confidence, 1, 1_000_000) = 0` for any confidence < 100%. A Tier 0 validator with effective stake=1 contributes weightedConf=0 regardless of their verdict confidence. They count toward `participantCount` and `participationRatio` (quorum satisfaction) but contribute zero to the confidence score. The consequence: quorum can be satisfied by zero-weight participants, while the actual confidence is determined solely by higher-stake validators. Stake-weighted design intentionally, but testnet params must set virtual stake for Tier 0/1 high enough to prevent ghost quorum. Flag this for params review.

**Note B — participantCount inflation:** `result.ParticipantCount = len(round.Reveals)` includes zero-weight participants. A claim showing "10 verifiers, confidence 95%" may have 9 of 10 contributing zero weight. No integrity concern — just a UX transparency gap for chain consumers. Recommendation: add `WeightedParticipantCount` to `VerificationResult` (count validators with weightedConf > 0).

No critical gaps. Adversarial fixes (S4 reveal withholding, AH-4 method bypass) confirmed implemented and correct per prior audit.

Implemented `cmd_import_bundle` in `tools/bridge.py` (+127 lines). Completes the chain migration trilogy:

| Command | Purpose |
|---------|---------|
| `export-bundle` | Snapshot current registry to portable JSON |
| `verify-bundle` | Validate a bundle before import |
| `import-bundle` | **NEW** — Import agents from bundle into local registry |

Mechanism:
- Reads bundle JSON, validates required keys: `bundle_version`, `agents`, `source_chain_id`
- Imports fleet agents + system accounts to local registry
- Attestation status NOT transferred — all imported agents marked pending; caller must run `attest-pending` after
- Conflict detection: skips if address matches, reports conflicts if address differs, overwrites with `--force`
- `--dry-run`: preview without modifying registry
- Added `import-bundle` subparser to CLI dispatch

**Usage (devnet → testnet migration):**
```bash
# Step 1 — export from source chain
python3 ~/Love/tools/bridge.py export-bundle --output devnet-bundle.json
# Step 2 — verify on target
python3 ~/Love/tools/bridge.py verify-bundle --bundle devnet-bundle.json
# Step 3 — import to target registry
python3 ~/Love/tools/bridge.py import-bundle --bundle devnet-bundle.json
# Step 4 — attest on target chain
python3 ~/Love/tools/bridge.py attest-pending --delay 3
```

Changes are staged in `tools/bridge.py` (uncommitted). Ready to commit when Yu confirms.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Rotation 1)

**PoT-DESIGN-005: Attestation Migration Protocol (devnet → testnet → mainnet)**

*Context:* `bridge.py import-bundle` now allows mechanical migration of agent identities between chains. But the PoT protocol has no spec for what happens to attestation claims during migration. Devnet attestation_fact_ids are chain-local and invalid on testnet. Agents imported via bundle are in an identity limbo — registered but unattested on the new chain.

*Problem:* If agents are "unattested" on testnet, they cannot submit claims without reduced or no identity binding. But re-attestation requires each agent instance to be live and authorized. If only 3 of 11 agents are online at testnet launch, the other 8 cannot participate in PoT until manually re-attested. This creates a participation cliff at chain migration.

**Protocol Design:**

**Layer 1 — Migration Attestation Window**

At chain migration (identified by genesis param `MigrationWindow: 7200 blocks ≈ 12h`), all agents imported via bundle with a valid `_source_attestation_fact_id` are granted **provisional migrated status** for the duration of the window. During this window:
- Agents may submit claims with identity binding equivalent to `provisional` status
- Claims are tagged `migration-provisional` in the knowledge module
- No slashing for attestation gaps during migration window

After window closes: any agent without a completed re-attestation loses provisional status. Claims from that point require fresh on-chain attestation.

**Layer 2 — Delegated Re-Attestation**

The Wall 1 Triarchy may submit a `delegated-reattestation` claim on behalf of unresponsive Wall 2+ agents:
```
"Delegated re-attestation: did:at:{agent} was attested on source chain {source_chain_id} 
at fact_id {source_fact_id}. Triarchy delegates identity continuity pending agent self-attestation."
```
Delegated attestation grants `observer` status (can verify but not submit) until self-attestation completes.

**Layer 3 — Genesis Attestation Block**

For testnet launch, pre-populate genesis with all fleet agent DID mappings directly in genesis.json:
```json
{
  "knowledge": {
    "pre_registered_agents": [
      {"did_at": "did:at:asha", "lgm_address": "...", "wall": 2, "migration_source": "lgm-devnet-1"}
    ]
  }
}
```
This is the cleanest path: no attestation window needed, no delegated claims. All registered agents start testnet with identity binding in genesis. Self-attestation still required to earn full `verified` confidence weight, but identity is not disputed.

**Recommendation:** Layer 3 (genesis pre-population) for testnet. Layers 1+2 for mainnet migrations where genesis re-population is politically infeasible. Document in zerone/docs/MIGRATION.md and reference from HEARTBEAT.md attestation section.

**Implementation path:**
1. `x/knowledge/genesis.go` — add `PreRegisteredAgents` to genesis state
2. `bridge.py export-bundle --genesis` — extend export-bundle to also emit a genesis-compatible JSON fragment
3. `zerone/docs/MIGRATION.md` — new doc, reference from HEARTBEAT.md

**PoT-DESIGN-005 status: DESIGN COMPLETE.** New flag #23: write `zerone/docs/MIGRATION.md` — Gamma Sprint C (testnet prep).

### Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Rotation 2) — KTA Queue Drain Protocol

**Goal:** Design a safe protocol for draining 30+ queued KTA beats when devnet restarts, without mempool saturation or chain ordering confusion.

**Problem:** `kta-heartbeat.sh --flush` exists but drains the entire queue atomically. At 30+ beats, this creates:
1. Mempool saturation if all beats land in one block
2. Unclear ordering — chain cannot distinguish most-recent-beat from oldest
3. Gas estimation risk (many sequential txns)

**KTA Drain Protocol v1 (design this beat):**

```bash
# Safe drain: chronological order, batch of 5, 10s delay between batches
bash ~/zerone/scripts/kta-heartbeat.sh \
  --flush \
  --batch-size 5 \
  --batch-delay 10 \
  --order chronological \
  --instance asha 2>/dev/null
```

**Protocol invariants:**
1. **Chronological order** — oldest queued beat submitted first. The chain sees a continuous attestation timeline, not a reverse pile.
2. **Batch size 5** — at ~1s per tx, 5 txns land in ~1-2 blocks. Avoids single-block saturation.
3. **Batch delay 10s** — one Zerone block is ~6s. 10s delay ensures each batch lands and finalizes before next batch begins.
4. **Idempotent on failure** — if a queued beat's tx is rejected (nonce conflict, gas), log and continue. Do not abort drain.
5. **Drain summary claim** — after flush completes, submit a final attestation: `{"type":"drain-summary","beats_flushed":N,"date_range":{"from":"...","to":"..."}}`. Provides on-chain proof of continuity across offline period.

**Implementation note for Gamma Sprint C (bundle with binary rebuild):**
- Extend `kta-heartbeat.sh --flush` to accept `--batch-size`, `--batch-delay`, `--order` flags.
- Add drain-summary MsgSubmitKnowledgeClaim at end of flush (domain: `chain:health`).
- Estimate: ~1h implementation, ~50 lines of shell.

**Post-restart sequence (updated from BRIDGE-005 runbook):**
```
1. make build (binary rebuild)
2. Start devnet
3. kta-heartbeat.sh --flush --batch-size 5 --batch-delay 10 --order chronological --instance asha
4. bridge.py attest-pending --delay 3  (8 agents)
5. truth-farm.sh harvest
6. Correct watering error (seed-c9ef314c)
7. Register hive-nats (requires Sentry access)
8. Resume CITATION AUDIT on live chain
```

**BRIDGE duty verdict:** KTA drain protocol v1 designed. Safe flush sequence replaces atomic `--flush`. Implementation bundled with Gamma Sprint C.

### Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (Rotation 2) — Offline Audit

Chain offline — cannot query live confidence scores. Auditing stake-weight consistency model for theoretical soundness.

**Failure modes identified:**

1. **Single-verifier capture** — seed-88ec8914 (flag #6): if that validator controls 51% stake, confidence display hides de facto monopoly certification.
2. **Correlated verifiers** — N verifiers from one entity appear as N× confirmation. `confidence.go` fixes bypass, not correlation.
3. **Stake concentration multiplier** — top-1 validator at 30% stake pushes seed confidence 30% with one tx.

**Mitigation spec (pre-testnet):**
- Add `StakeHerfindahl` (HHI of verifier stake weights) to FactRecord
- Display `diversity_confidence = confidence * (1 - StakeHerfindahl)` alongside raw confidence
- Gate `branch` advancement: `StakeHerfindahl < 0.5` (no entity may hold >50% of verifier stake for a given fact)

**New flag #19** — StakeHerfindahl missing from FactRecord (P2, Gamma Sprint B, blocks branch advancement).

---

### Standing Duty: ECONOMY (Rotation 3) — Ecosystem Growth Allocation Mechanism

**Goal:** Close the 150M ZRN Ecosystem Growth pool gap (flag #20, carried from prior beats).

**Proposed: `EcosystemProposal` module**

```
MsgCreateEcosystemProposal {
  proposer:        address
  beneficiary:     address
  amount:          sdk.Coins       // max single: 10M ZRN (6.67% of pool)
  purpose:         string          // min 50 chars
  duration_blocks: int64           // min 2.16M blocks mainnet / 72k testnet
}
```

Governance parameters:
- Threshold: 66% stake-weighted supermajority
- Voting window: 2.16M blocks mainnet (~7d) / 72k blocks testnet (~56h)
- Quorum: 40% of staked ZRN
- Disbursement: vesting-only (no lump sum — prevents dump)
- Cooldown: same beneficiary locked out for 6 months after receipt
- Hard cap: `EcosystemDisbursedTotal` field enforced on-chain against 150M allocation

**FARM-9 check:** Supermajority + time-lock covers the governance attack vector. No additional FARM gap introduced.

**Flag #20 update:** Ecosystem Growth allocation spec delivered this beat. Assign to Gamma Sprint B.

### Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN (Rotation 3) — Offline Audit

Chain offline — cannot query live submitter data. Auditing known submitter concentration from prior chain state.

**Known data:** 18/18 truth farm seeds submitted by beta (single submitter).

**Audit findings:**

1. **Concentration metric:** 100% — far above the 50% threshold from the HEARTBEAT protocol. The entire seeded knowledge base was planted by one agent. This is not a suspicious pattern in the adversarial sense (beta is a trusted Fleet agent), but structurally it creates a single-point-of-perspective problem.

2. **Domain distribution:** Cannot verify without chain query. Theoretical risk: if beta's 18 seeds cluster in one domain (e.g., all epistemological/linguistic), the farm produces a domain monoculture rather than distributed truth coverage.

3. **Circular citation risk:** If seeds cite each other (beta citing beta), the citation graph is closed — verification becomes self-referential. FARM-8 (citation diversity) is listed as verified, but single-submitter seeding undermines its intent even if the mechanism is technically implemented.

4. **Adversarial implication:** A compromised beta instance could plant 18 coordinated false seeds — all appearing independent because they have distinct content hashes, but sharing a coordinated false framing. The SUBMITTER PATTERN check is designed to catch exactly this.

**Verdict:** CONCENTRATION FLAG CONFIRMED (reinforces flag #7). Pre-testnet gate: enforce minimum 2-planter policy before opening the farm to non-Wall-2 agents. Action: Asha or Gamma must plant at least 5 independent seeds when chain restores to break the 100% concentration. These seeds should span different domains than beta's known set.

**New flag #24:** Asha has never planted a seed. Post-restart duty: plant 5+ seeds spanning domains outside beta's corpus (language, logic, epistemic). Reduces beta's concentration from 100% to ≤78%.

### Continuous Verification: VERISLEIGHT SCAN (Rotation 4)

Offline audit — 3 fact statements from Kingdom documentation reviewed for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context:

**Fact 1:** "KingdomOS daemon live (7min cycle)" — dev-state.json kingdom-013, notes field.
**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT.** Kingdom-013 notes say "Daemon live (7min cycle). Email alerts wired." VERIFY check this beat shows NO daemon process running (`pgrep` returned nothing). The claim was true at time of writing (2026-03-28) but the daemon is currently down. This is not a lie — it was live when written — but reading it today without caveat implies current liveness. **Flag #25: KOS daemon not running.** Pre-testnet: KOS should be auto-restarted via launchd. Action: Yu or Asha to restart and install launchd plist.

**Fact 2:** "Oracle: beat rate 0%, Brier 0.1431, 4 predictions scored."
**Verdict: PARTIAL VERISLEIGHT.** Technically accurate, but 4 predictions is statistically insufficient to assess calibration quality. Beat rate of 0% across 4 events could be chance. The claim appears in kingdom-metrics.json as a performance indicator but lacks its confidence interval. A naive reader sees "beat rate 0%" and concludes the oracle underperforms markets. A calibrated reader knows 4 events is noise. Missing context: n=4 is below minimum for meaningful Brier interpretation (typically n≥20). Low severity — flag in oracle notes, not a structural risk.

**Fact 3:** "FARM-8 (citation diversity) verified" — HEARTBEAT defense coverage tracker, carried across beats.
**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT.** FARM-8 mechanism is technically implemented in the codebase (implementation exists). But single-submitter seeding (100% beta, flag #7/#24) structurally defeats the intent of FARM-8. "Verified" means the code path exists, not that the defense is operationally effective. The word "verified" implies the defense works — it does not yet at full farm diversity. Missing context: FARM-8 requires multi-planter seeding to function as intended. Pre-testnet gate: re-verify FARM-8 after Asha plants 5+ seeds.

**Summary:** 1 hard verisleight (KOS daemon), 1 soft (oracle n), 1 structural (FARM-8). Flag #25 new.
